The Committee found that this was caused by Hudson's 'unjust trading in those parts to the prejudice of the great ones there, and for his soe doing might justly deserve imprisonment'; and he was therefore condemned to make good the amount to the Company.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-120" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-120" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Evidently the rupee is taken at 2<em>s.</em> 3<em>d.</em></span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-121" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-121" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">In <em>Travels of Venetians in Persia</em> (Hakluyt Society, 1873) mention is made (p. 56) of 'Chianer, whence come the Canary silks', and (p. 112) of a fortress called 'Canar', where 'Canarian' silk is made. This place seems to have been in or near the valley of the Aras; possibly it is the modern Kainar, 25 miles north-west of Khoi.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-122" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-122" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Tavernier mentions that calicoes were brought from long distances to be bleached at Broach, as the water of the river was supposed to possess special qualities for that purpose. He also praises the baftas made there.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-123" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-123" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">For previous attempts of the Dutch to open up trade in Sind see the 1630-33 volume, p. 207.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-124" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-124" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">An allusion, of course, to the ‘wise men of Gotham’, who were so very foolish.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-125" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-125" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">In the list of packet his name is given as ‘Odor Butt’, i. e. Adhar Bhatt.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-126" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-126" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">For these schemes of founding colonies in Mauritius and Madagascar see <em>The Court Minutes of the East India Company, 1635–39</em>, and the succeeding volumes.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-127" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-127" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Kārwār, in North Kanara, 50 miles below Goa. Fryer, who was there in 1675, describes the then English factory as standing 'on a delicate mead, on the ground of — Cutteen, Esq., a Cornish gentleman, who had it by grant from the King of Visiapour . . . seated on an arm of the river, surveying a pleasant island stored with game' (p. 146).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-128" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-128" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">A jeweller's weight (<em>tanka</em>), equivalent to four <em>māshas</em>.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-129" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-129" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">In 1633 instructions had been given not to interfere with Malabar vessels (see the 1630–33 volume, p. 272).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-130" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-130" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">In Moll's map of Arabia (1711) a town of ‘Dofar’ is shown about half-way along the coast from Aden to Ras-al-hadd, and a little to the west of Murbāt. It is probably represented by the Hāfah of modern maps.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-131" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-131" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Gilles Régimont, or Rézimont, already mentioned on p. 59. The name of his ship appears to be Dutch (<em>Eendracht, Concord</em>). For his transportation to England and ultimate <span class="folio-turn">[p. 212]</span> release there (June, 1641) see <em>Court Minutes of the East India Company, 1640–43</em>, p. 56, &amp;c. Rézimont thereupon made a fresh voyage to the East, some details of which will be found in François Cauche's <em>Relations veritables et curieuses de l'Isle de Madagascar</em> (Paris, 1651). His arrival at St. Helena, homeward-bound, in a vessel of 150 tons, 14 guns, and 55 men, in March, 1644, is recorded by Captain William Minors, who was then returning from Bantam to England in the <em>Mary</em> (<em>O.C.</em> 1868).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-132" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-132" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body"><em>Armada de Alto Bordo</em>, the fleet of ‘tall’ ships, meaning the Portuguese war vessels as distinguished from their coasting craft.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-133" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-133" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">See Gray’s edition of Pyrard de Laval, vol. ii. pp. 10, 51.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-134" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-134" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Dutch <em>fluit</em>, a flyboat or flute, i. e. a fast-sailing vessel used for the conveyance of merchandise. Cf. the 1630–33 volume, p. 184.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-135" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-135" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">Taiwan (the Island of Formosa).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-136" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-136" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">For a farmān obtained by the Dutch from the Sultan of Yemen in December, 1638, see the <em>Hague Transcripts</em>, series i. vol. xi. no. 367.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-137" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-137" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Westerwold: see his letter in the <em>Hague Transcripts</em> (series i. vol. xi. no. 358).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-138" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-138" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">For the Dutch reply see <em>Hague Transcripts</em>, series i. vol. xi. no. 363.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-139" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-139" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">Trincomali was captured by Caen’s fleet on May 2, 1639 N.S. (<em>Hague Transcripts</em>, series i. vol. xi. no. 360).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-140" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-140" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">See the <em>Hague Transcripts</em>, series i. vol. xi. no. 364.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-141" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-141" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Anton Van Diemen : see the previous volume, p. 143.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-142" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-142" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">For a second copy see the <em>O.C. Duplicates</em>.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-143" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-143" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">‘50,000’ in the duplicate.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-144" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-144" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">‘3,000’ in the duplicate.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-145" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-145" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">See <em>Corpus Diplomaticum Neerlando-Indicum</em>, by J. E. Heeres, p. 332.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-146" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-146" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Hence his title of Mullā.</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-147" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-147" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Probably Chicacole, in Ganjām district, famous for its fine muslins. The Dutch procured a farmān for trade there in December, 1639 (Heeres, <em>op. cit.</em>, p. 334).</span></li><li id="sec-06-fn-148" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-06-fn-148" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Pinson sailed for England in the <em>William</em>. These papers were handed to him for delivery to the Company, and he signed the list in acknowledgement of their receipt. Another list (<em>O.C.</em> 1749) of documents sent home (by the same ship?) includes three bundles of ‘Coast letters’.</span></li></ol></section>
